# ONNX 部署指南（Ultralytics 官方文档归档）

> **系列定位**：本文是「部署格式专题」之一 —— 跨框架交换格式，几乎所有转换链路（RKNN / TensorRT / OpenVINO）的起点。内容为 Ultralytics 官方英文文档完整归档，与中文实操文章互补。总目录见 [README 导读](README.md)。
>
> - 中文实操路线：[Yolo模型的转换与rknn-toolkit相关工具链的使用](YOLO模型的转换与rknn-toolkit相关工具链的使用.md)
> - 关联阅读：[yolo模型推理与部署详解](YOLO模型推理与部署详解.md) · [模型量化深度解析](模型量化深度解析.md)

---

id="onnx-export-for-yolo26-models" class="group relative">ONNX Export for YOLO26 Models<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#onnx-export-for-yolo26-models">#</a></h1><details class="admon admon-success" open=""><summary class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-lightbulb admon-icon" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M15 14c.2-1 .7-1.7 1.5-2.5 1-.9 1.5-2.2 1.5-3.5A6 6 0 0 0 6 8c0 1 .2 2.2 1.5 3.5.7.7 1.3 1.5 1.5 2.5"></path><path d="M9 18h6"></path><path d="M10 22h4"></path></svg><span>~43% faster inference.</span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right admon-chevron" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg></summary><div class="admon-content"><ul>
<li>Exporting the Ultralytics YOLO26 model to ONNX can deliver up to a 43% boost in inference speed, enabling faster and more efficient deployment.</li>
</ul></div></details><p>Often, when deploying <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/computer-vision-cv" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">computer vision</a> models, you&#x27;ll need a model format that&#x27;s both flexible and compatible with multiple platforms.</p>
<p>Exporting <a href="https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Ultralytics YOLO26</a> models to ONNX format streamlines deployment and ensures optimal performance across various environments. This guide will show you how to easily convert your YOLO26 models to ONNX and enhance their scalability and effectiveness in real-world applications.</p>
<div align="center">
  <br/>
  <iframe allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" allowFullScreen="" class="aspect-video w-full border-0" loading="lazy" src="https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/cxU5E2SkivU" title="YouTube video player" frameBorder="0">
  </iframe>
  <br/>
  <strong>Watch:</strong> Ultralytics YOLO26 vs Ultralytics YOLO11 ONNX Inference Test | ~43% Faster Inference with YOLO26 🚀
</div>
<h2 id="onnx-and-onnx-runtime" class="group relative">ONNX and ONNX Runtime<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#onnx-and-onnx-runtime">#</a></h2>
<p><a href="https://onnx.ai/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ONNX</a>, which stands for Open <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/neural-network-nn" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Neural Network</a> Exchange, is a community project that Facebook and Microsoft initially developed. The ongoing development of ONNX is a collaborative effort supported by various organizations like IBM, Amazon (through AWS), and Google. The project aims to create an open file format designed to represent <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/machine-learning-ml" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">machine learning</a> models in a way that allows them to be used across different AI frameworks and hardware.</p>
<p>ONNX models can be used to transition between different frameworks seamlessly. For instance, a <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/deep-learning-dl" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">deep learning</a> model trained in PyTorch can be exported to ONNX format and then easily imported into TensorFlow.</p>
<p align="center">
  <img alt="ONNX model portability across deep learning frameworks" decoding="async" loading="lazy" src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/onnx-model-portability.avif" width="100%"/>
</p>
<p>Alternatively, ONNX models can be used with ONNX Runtime. <a href="https://onnxruntime.ai/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ONNX Runtime</a> is a versatile cross-platform accelerator for machine learning models that is compatible with frameworks like PyTorch, <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/tensorflow" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">TensorFlow</a>, scikit-learn, etc.</p>
<p>ONNX Runtime optimizes the execution of ONNX models by leveraging hardware-specific capabilities. This optimization allows the models to run efficiently and with high performance on various hardware platforms, including CPUs, GPUs, and specialized accelerators.</p>
<p align="center">
  <img alt="ONNX Runtime cross-platform inference acceleration" decoding="async" loading="lazy" src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/onnx-and-onnx-runtime.avif" width="100%"/>
</p>
<p>Whether used independently or in tandem with ONNX Runtime, ONNX provides a flexible solution for machine learning <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/model-deployment" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">model deployment</a> and compatibility.</p>
<h2 id="key-features-of-onnx-models" class="group relative">Key Features of ONNX Models<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#key-features-of-onnx-models">#</a></h2>
<p>The ability of ONNX to handle various formats can be attributed to the following key features:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p><strong>Common Model Representation</strong>: ONNX defines a common set of operators (like convolutions, layers, etc.) and a standard data format. When a model is converted to ONNX format, its architecture and weights are translated into this common representation. This uniformity ensures that the model can be understood by any framework that supports ONNX.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Versioning and Backward Compatibility</strong>: ONNX maintains a versioning system for its operators. This ensures that even as the standard evolves, models created in older versions remain usable. Backward compatibility is a crucial feature that prevents models from becoming obsolete quickly.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Graph-based Model Representation</strong>: ONNX represents models as computational graphs. This graph-based structure is a universal way of representing machine learning models, where nodes represent operations or computations, and edges represent the tensors flowing between them. This format is easily adaptable to various frameworks which also represent models as graphs.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Tools and Ecosystem</strong>: There is a rich ecosystem of tools around ONNX that assist in model conversion, visualization, and optimization. These tools make it easier for developers to work with ONNX models and to convert models between different frameworks seamlessly.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="common-usage-of-onnx" class="group relative">Common Usage of ONNX<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#common-usage-of-onnx">#</a></h2>
<p>Before we jump into how to export YOLO26 models to the ONNX format, let&#x27;s take a look at where ONNX models are usually used.</p>
<h3 id="cpu-deployment" class="group relative">CPU Deployment<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#cpu-deployment">#</a></h3>
<p>ONNX models are often deployed on CPUs due to their compatibility with ONNX Runtime. This runtime is optimized for CPU execution. It significantly improves inference speed and makes real-time CPU deployments feasible.</p>
<h3 id="supported-deployment-options" class="group relative">Supported Deployment Options<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#supported-deployment-options">#</a></h3>
<p>While ONNX models are commonly used on CPUs, they can also be deployed on the following platforms:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p><strong>GPU Acceleration</strong>: ONNX fully supports GPU acceleration, particularly NVIDIA CUDA. For native training and inference on AMD GPUs, use <a href="/integrations/amd">PyTorch ROCm</a>; an ONNX file still requires an AMD-compatible runtime.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Edge and Mobile Devices</strong>: ONNX extends to edge and mobile devices, perfect for on-device and real-time inference scenarios. It&#x27;s lightweight and compatible with edge hardware, and serves as the basis for vendor NPU formats such as <a href="/integrations/ascend">Huawei Ascend</a>, <a href="/integrations/qnn">Qualcomm QNN</a> for Snapdragon devices, and <a href="/integrations/rockchip-rknn">RKNN</a> for Rockchip NPUs.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Web Browsers</strong>: ONNX can run directly in web browsers, powering interactive and dynamic web-based AI applications.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="supported-tasks" class="group relative">Supported Tasks<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#supported-tasks">#</a></h2>
<p>ONNX export supports all seven Ultralytics tasks. Semantic segmentation and depth estimation are available only with YOLO26, the only family that ships those heads.</p>

<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Task</th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolov8">YOLOv8</a></th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolo11">YOLO11</a></th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolo26">YOLO26</a></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/detect">Detect</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/segment">Segment</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/semantic">Semantic</a></td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/depth">Depth</a></td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/classify">Classify</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/pose">Pose</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/obb">OBB</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<h2 id="exporting-yolo26-models-to-onnx" class="group relative">Exporting YOLO26 Models to ONNX<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#exporting-yolo26-models-to-onnx">#</a></h2>
<p>You can expand model compatibility and deployment flexibility by converting YOLO26 models to ONNX format. <a href="/models/yolo26">Ultralytics YOLO26</a> provides a straightforward export process that can significantly enhance your model&#x27;s performance across different platforms.</p>

<h3 id="usage" class="group relative">Usage<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#usage">#</a></h3>
<p>Before diving into the usage instructions, be sure to check out the range of <a href="/models">YOLO26 models offered by Ultralytics</a>. This will help you choose the most appropriate model for your project requirements.</p>
<p>The ONNX format supports the <a href="/modes/export">Export</a>, <a href="/modes/predict">Predict</a>, and <a href="/modes/val">Validate</a> modes. Export your model, then load the exported model to run inference or validate its accuracy.</p><section class="admon admon-example"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" # Load a YOLO26 model

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n.pt&quot;)

# Export the model to ONNX format

model.export(format=&quot;onnx&quot;)  # creates &#x27;yolo26n.onnx&#x27;

# Export an INT8-quantized ONNX model with calibration data

model.export(format=&quot;onnx&quot;, quantize=8, data=&quot;coco8.yaml&quot;)  # creates &#x27;yolo26n_int8.onnx&#x27;</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><section class="admon ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load the exported ONNX model

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n.onnx&quot;)

# Validate accuracy on the COCO8 dataset

metrics = model.val(data=&quot;coco8.yaml&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><h3 id="export-arguments" class="group relative">Export Arguments<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#export-arguments">#</a></h3>
<p>When exporting your YOLO26 model to ONNX format, you can customize the process using various arguments to optimize for your specific deployment needs:</p>

<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Argument</th><th>Type</th><th>Default</th><th>Description</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>format</code></td><td><code>str</code></td><td><code>&#x27;onnx&#x27;</code></td><td>Target format for the exported model, defining compatibility with various deployment environments.</td></tr><tr><td><code>imgsz</code></td><td><code>int</code> or <code>tuple</code></td><td><code>640</code></td><td>Desired image size for the model input. Can be an integer for square images or a tuple <code>(height, width)</code> for specific dimensions.</td></tr><tr><td><code>quantize</code></td><td><code>int</code> or <code>str</code></td><td><code>None</code></td><td>Quantization precision: <code>16</code> (FP16) or <code>8</code> (INT8 static quantization with ONNX Runtime using calibration images from <code>data</code>, producing an <code>_int8.onnx</code> model); <code>32</code>/unset is FP32. Replaces the deprecated <code>half</code>/<code>int8</code> flags.</td></tr><tr><td><code>data</code></td><td><code>str</code></td><td><code>None</code></td><td>Dataset YAML used for INT8 calibration; classification instead takes a dataset directory or a built-in dataset name. If omitted with <code>quantize=8</code>, Ultralytics selects the default calibration dataset for the model task.</td></tr><tr><td><code>fraction</code></td><td><code>float</code></td><td><code>1.0</code></td><td>Fraction of calibration images to use for INT8 quantization.</td></tr><tr><td><code>dynamic</code></td><td><code>bool</code></td><td><code>False</code></td><td>Allows dynamic input sizes, enhancing flexibility in handling varying image dimensions.</td></tr><tr><td><code>simplify</code></td><td><code>bool</code></td><td><code>True</code></td><td>Simplifies the model graph with <code>onnxslim</code>, potentially improving performance and compatibility.</td></tr><tr><td><code>opset</code></td><td><code>int</code></td><td><code>None</code></td><td>Specifies the ONNX opset version for compatibility with different ONNX parsers and runtimes. If not set, uses the latest supported version.</td></tr><tr><td><code>nms</code></td><td><code>bool</code></td><td><code>False</code></td><td>Adds Non-Maximum Suppression (NMS), essential for accurate and efficient detection post-processing.</td></tr><tr><td><code>batch</code></td><td><code>int</code></td><td><code>1</code></td><td>Specifies export model batch inference size or the max number of images the exported model will process concurrently in <code>predict</code> mode.</td></tr><tr><td><code>device</code></td><td><code>str</code></td><td><code>None</code></td><td>Specifies the device for exporting: GPU (<code>device=0</code>), CPU (<code>device=cpu</code>), MPS for Apple silicon (<code>device=mps</code>).</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>For more details about the export process, visit the <a href="/modes/export">Ultralytics documentation page on exporting</a>.</p>
<h2 id="deploying-exported-yolo26-onnx-models" class="group relative">Deploying Exported YOLO26 ONNX Models<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#deploying-exported-yolo26-onnx-models">#</a></h2>
<p>Once you&#x27;ve successfully exported your Ultralytics YOLO26 models to ONNX format, the next step is deploying these models in various environments. For detailed instructions on deploying your ONNX models, take a look at the following resources:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p><strong><a href="https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/api/python/api_summary.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ONNX Runtime Python API Documentation</a></strong>: This guide provides essential information for loading and running ONNX models using ONNX Runtime.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong><a href="https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/tutorials/iot-edge/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Deploying on Edge Devices</a></strong>: Check out this docs page for different examples of deploying ONNX models on edge.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong><a href="https://github.com/onnx/tutorials" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ONNX Tutorials on GitHub</a></strong>: A collection of comprehensive tutorials that cover various aspects of using and implementing ONNX models in different scenarios.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong><a href="/guides/triton-inference-server">Triton Inference Server</a></strong>: Learn how to deploy your ONNX models with NVIDIA&#x27;s Triton Inference Server for high-performance, scalable deployments.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="summary" class="group relative">Summary<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#summary">#</a></h2>
<p>In this guide, you&#x27;ve learned how to export Ultralytics YOLO26 models to ONNX format to increase their interoperability and performance across various platforms. You were also introduced to the ONNX Runtime and ONNX deployment options.</p>
<p>ONNX export is just one of many <a href="/modes/export">export formats</a> supported by Ultralytics YOLO26, allowing you to deploy your models in virtually any environment. Depending on your specific needs, you might also want to explore other export options like <a href="/integrations/tensorrt">TensorRT</a> for maximum GPU performance or <a href="/integrations/coreml">CoreML</a> for Apple devices.</p>
<p>For further details on usage, visit the <a href="https://onnx.ai/onnx/intro/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ONNX official documentation</a>.</p>
<p>Also, if you&#x27;d like to know more about other Ultralytics YOLO26 integrations, visit our <a href="/integrations">integration guide page</a>. You&#x27;ll find plenty of useful resources and insights there.</p>
<h2 id="faq" class="group relative">FAQ<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#faq">#</a></h2></div><script type="application/ld+json">{"@context":"https://schema.org","@type":"FAQPage","mainEntity":[{"@type":"Question","name":"How do I export YOLO26 models to ONNX format using Ultralytics?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"To export your YOLO26 models to ONNX format using Ultralytics, follow these steps: For more details, visit the export documentation."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"What are the advantages of using ONNX Runtime for deploying YOLO26 models?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Using ONNX Runtime for deploying YOLO26 models offers several advantages: Cross-platform compatibility: ONNX Runtime supports various platforms, such as Windows, macOS, and Linux, ensuring your models run smoothly across different environments. Hardware acceleration: ONNX Runtime can leverage hardware-specific optimizations for CPUs, GPUs, and dedicated accelerators, providing high-performance inference. Framework interoperability: Models trained in popular frameworks like PyTorch or TensorFlow can be easily converted to ONNX format and run using ONNX Runtime. Performance optimization: ONNX Runtime can provide up to 3x CPU speedup compared to native PyTorch models, making it ideal for deployment scenarios where GPU resources are limited. Learn more by checking the ONNX Runtime documentation."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"What deployment options are available for YOLO26 models exported to ONNX?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"YOLO26 models exported to ONNX can be deployed on various platforms including: CPUs: Utilizing ONNX Runtime for optimized CPU inference. GPUs: Leveraging NVIDIA CUDA for high-performance GPU acceleration. Edge devices: Running lightweight models on edge and mobile devices for real-time, on-device inference. Web browsers: Executing models directly within web browsers for interactive web-based applications. Cloud services: Deploying on cloud platforms that support ONNX format for scalable inference. For more information, explore our guide on model deployment options."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"Why should I use ONNX format for Ultralytics YOLO26 models?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Using ONNX format for Ultralytics YOLO26 models provides numerous benefits: Interoperability: ONNX allows models to be transferred between different machine learning frameworks seamlessly. Performance Optimization: ONNX Runtime can enhance model performance by utilizing hardware-specific optimizations. Flexibility: ONNX supports various deployment environments, enabling you to use the same model on different platforms without modification. Standardization: ONNX provides a standardized format that is widely supported across the industry, ensuring long-term compatibility. Refer to the comprehensive guide on exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"How can I troubleshoot issues when exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"When exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX, you might encounter common issues such as mismatched dependencies or unsupported operations. To troubleshoot these problems: Verify that you have the correct version of required dependencies installed. Check the official ONNX documentation for supported operators and features. Review the error messages for clues and consult the Ultralytics Common Issues guide. Try using different export arguments like simplify=True or adjusting the opset version. For dynamic input size issues, set dynamic=True during export. If issues persist, contact Ultralytics support for further assistance."}}]}</script><section><div class="@container/main flex min-w-0 flex-1 flex-col md:p-6 mt-2 max-w-none gap-3 p-0"><ul class="grid stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load a YOLO26 model

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n.pt&quot;)

# Export the model to ONNX format

model.export(format=&quot;onnx&quot;)  # creates &#x27;yolo26n.onnx&#x27;

# Load the exported ONNX model

onnx_model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n.onnx&quot;)

# Run inference

results = onnx_model(&quot;https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><p>For more details, visit the <a href="/modes/export">export documentation</a>.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="what-are-the-advantages-of-using-onnx-runtime-for-deploying-yolo26-models"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="what-are-the-advantages-of-using-onnx-runtime-for-deploying-yolo26-models-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">What are the advantages of using ONNX Runtime for deploying YOLO26 models?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="what-are-the-advantages-of-using-onnx-runtime-for-deploying-yolo26-models-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>Using ONNX Runtime for deploying YOLO26 models offers several advantages:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Cross-platform compatibility</strong>: ONNX Runtime supports various platforms, such as Windows, macOS, and Linux, ensuring your models run smoothly across different environments.</li>
<li><strong>Hardware acceleration</strong>: ONNX Runtime can leverage hardware-specific optimizations for CPUs, GPUs, and dedicated accelerators, providing high-performance inference.</li>
<li><strong>Framework interoperability</strong>: Models trained in popular frameworks like <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/pytorch" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">PyTorch</a> or TensorFlow can be easily converted to ONNX format and run using ONNX Runtime.</li>
<li><strong>Performance optimization</strong>: ONNX Runtime can provide up to 3x CPU speedup compared to native PyTorch models, making it ideal for deployment scenarios where GPU resources are limited.</li>
</ul>
<p>Learn more by checking the <a href="https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/api/python/api_summary.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ONNX Runtime documentation</a>.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="what-deployment-options-are-available-for-yolo26-models-exported-to-onnx"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="what-deployment-options-are-available-for-yolo26-models-exported-to-onnx-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">What deployment options are available for YOLO26 models exported to ONNX?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="what-deployment-options-are-available-for-yolo26-models-exported-to-onnx-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>YOLO26 models exported to ONNX can be deployed on various platforms including:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>CPUs</strong>: Utilizing ONNX Runtime for optimized CPU inference.</li>
<li><strong>GPUs</strong>: Leveraging NVIDIA CUDA for high-performance GPU acceleration.</li>
<li><strong>Edge devices</strong>: Running lightweight models on edge and mobile devices for real-time, on-device inference.</li>
<li><strong>Web browsers</strong>: Executing models directly within web browsers for interactive web-based applications.</li>
<li><strong>Cloud services</strong>: Deploying on cloud platforms that support ONNX format for scalable inference.</li>
</ul>
<p>For more information, explore our guide on <a href="/guides/model-deployment-options">model deployment options</a>.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="why-should-i-use-onnx-format-for-ultralytics-yolo26-models"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="why-should-i-use-onnx-format-for-ultralytics-yolo26-models-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">Why should I use ONNX format for Ultralytics YOLO26 models?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="why-should-i-use-onnx-format-for-ultralytics-yolo26-models-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>Using ONNX format for Ultralytics YOLO26 models provides numerous benefits:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Interoperability</strong>: ONNX allows models to be transferred between different machine learning frameworks seamlessly.</li>
<li><strong>Performance Optimization</strong>: ONNX Runtime can enhance model performance by utilizing hardware-specific optimizations.</li>
<li><strong>Flexibility</strong>: ONNX supports various deployment environments, enabling you to use the same model on different platforms without modification.</li>
<li><strong>Standardization</strong>: ONNX provides a standardized format that is widely supported across the industry, ensuring long-term compatibility.</li>
</ul>
<p>Refer to the comprehensive guide on <a href="/integrations/onnx">exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX</a>.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="how-can-i-troubleshoot-issues-when-exporting-yolo26-models-to-onnx"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="how-can-i-troubleshoot-issues-when-exporting-yolo26-models-to-onnx-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">How can I troubleshoot issues when exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="how-can-i-troubleshoot-issues-when-exporting-yolo26-models-to-onnx-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>When exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX, you might encounter common issues such as mismatched dependencies or unsupported operations. To troubleshoot these problems:</p>
<ol>
<li>Verify that you have the correct version of required dependencies installed.</li>
<li>Check the official <a href="https://onnx.ai/onnx/intro/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ONNX documentation</a> for supported operators and features.</li>
<li>Review the error messages for clues and consult the <a href="/guides/yolo-common-issues">Ultralytics Common Issues guide</a>.</li>
<li>Try using different export arguments like <code>simplify=True</code> or adjusting the <code>opset</code> version.</li>
<li>For dynamic input size issues, set <code>dynamic=True</code> during export.</li>
</ol>
<p>If issues persist, contact Ultralytics support for further assistance.</p></div></div></div></li></ul></div></section><div class="mt-16 space-y-3 text-right text-sm text-muted-foreground"><div Creators of YOLO.\\\"},{\\\"@type\\\":\\\"WebSite\\\",\\\"@id\\\":\\\"https://docs.ultralytics.com#website\\\",\\\"name\\\":\\\"Ultralytics Docs\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://docs.ultralytics.com\\\",\\\"publisher\\\":{\\\"@id\\\":\\\"https://www.ultralytics.com#organization\\\"}}]}\"}]\n30:T4ca,{\"@context\":\"https://schema.org\",\"@graph\":[{\"@type\":\"Article\",\"headline\":\"ONNX Export for YOLO26 Models\",\"url\":\"https://docs.ultralytics.com/integrations/onnx\",\"description\":\"Learn how to export YOLO26 models to ONNX format for flexible deployment across various platforms with enhanced performance.\",\"image\":\"https://i.ytimg.com/vi/cxU5E2SkivU/maxresdefault.jpg\",\"datePublished\":\"2024-01-25T21:13:58+03:00\",\"dateModified\":\"2026-08-09T09:41:54+06:00\",\"author\":{\"@type\":\"Organization\",\"@id\":\"https://www.ultralytics.com#organization\",\"name\":\"Ultralytics\",\"url\":\"https://www.ultralytics.com\"},\"publisher\":{\"@type\":\"Organization\",\"@id\":\"https://www.ultralytics.com#organization\",\"name\":\"Ultralytics\",\"url\":\"https://www.ultralytics.com\",\"logo\":{\"@type\":\"ImageObject\",\"url\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/assets/main/logo/Ultralytics-logomark-color.png\"}}},{\"@type\":\"BreadcrumbList\",\"itemListElement\":[{\"@type\":\"ListItem\",\"position\":1,\"name\":\"Ultralytics Docs\",\"item\":\"https://docs.ultralytics.com/\"},{\"@type\":\"ListItem\",\"position\":2,\"name\":\"Integrations\",\"item\":\"https://docs.ultralytics.com/integrations\"},{\"@type\":\"ListItem\",\"position\":3,\"name\":\"ONNX\",\"item\":\"https://docs.ultralytics.com/integrations/onnx\"}]}]}36:T4077,\n# ONNX Export for YOLO26 Models\n\n???+ tip \"~43% faster inference.\"\n\n    - Exporting the Ultralytics YOLO26 model to ONNX can deliver up to a 43% boost in inference speed, enabling faster and more efficient deployment.\n\nOften, when deploying [computer vision](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/computer-vision-cv) models, you'll need a model format that's both flexible and compatible with multiple platforms.\n\nExporting [Ultralytics YOLO26](https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics) models to ONNX format streamlines deployment and ensures optimal performance across various environments. This guide will show you how to easily convert your YOLO26 models to ONNX and enhance their scalability and effectiveness in real-world applications.\n\n\u003cp align=\"center\"\u003e\n  \u003cbr\u003e\n  \u003ciframe loading=\"lazy\" width=\"720\" height=\"405\" src=\"https://www.youtube.com/embed/cxU5E2SkivU\"\n    title=\"YouTube video player\" frameborder=\"0\"\n    allow=\"accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share\"\n    allowfullscreen\u003e\n  \u003c/iframe\u003e\n  \u003cbr\u003e\n  \u003cstrong\u003eWatch:\u003c/strong\u003e Ultralytics YOLO26 vs Ultralytics YOLO11 ONNX Inference Test | ~43% Faster Inference with YOLO26 🚀\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\n## ONNX and ONNX Runtime\n\n[ONNX](https://onnx.ai/), which stands for Open [Neural Network](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/neural-network-nn) Exchange, is a community project that Facebook and Microsoft initially developed. The ongoing development of ONNX is a collaborative effort supported by various organizations like IBM, Amazon (through AWS), and Google. The project aims to create an open file format designed to represent [machine learning](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/machine-learning-ml) models in a way that allows them to be used across different AI frameworks and hardware.\n\nONNX models can be used to transition between different frameworks seamlessly. For instance, a [deep learning](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/deep-learning-dl) model trained in PyTorch can be exported to ONNX format and then easily imported into TensorFlow.\n\n\u003cp align=\"center\"\u003e\n  \u003cimg width=\"100%\" src=\"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/onnx-model-portability.avif\" alt=\"ONNX model portability across deep learning frameworks\"\u003e\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\nAlternatively, ONNX models can be used with ONNX Runtime. [ONNX Runtime](https://onnxruntime.ai/) is a versatile cross-platform accelerator for machine learning models that is compatible with frameworks like PyTorch, [TensorFlow](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/tensorflow), scikit-learn, etc.\n\nONNX Runtime optimizes the execution of ONNX models by leveraging hardware-specific capabilities. This optimization allows the models to run efficiently and with high performance on various hardware platforms, including CPUs, GPUs, and specialized accelerators.\n\n\u003cp align=\"center\"\u003e\n  \u003cimg width=\"100%\" src=\"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/onnx-and-onnx-runtime.avif\" alt=\"ONNX Runtime cross-platform inference acceleration\"\u003e\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\nWhether used independently or in tandem with ONNX Runtime, ONNX provides a flexible solution for machine learning [model deployment](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/model-deployment) and compatibility.\n\n## Key Features of ONNX Models\n\nThe ability of ONNX to handle various formats can be attributed to the following key features:\n\n- **Common Model Representation**: ONNX defines a common set of operators (like convolutions, layers, etc.) and a standard data format. When a model is converted to ONNX format, its architecture and weights are translated into this common representation. This uniformity ensures that the model can be understood by any framework that supports ONNX.\n\n- **Versioning and Backward Compatibility**: ONNX maintains a versioning system for its operators. This ensures that even as the standard evolves, models created in older versions remain usable. Backward compatibility is a crucial feature that prevents models from becoming obsolete quickly.\n\n- **Graph-based Model Representation**: ONNX represents models as computational graphs. This graph-based structure is a universal way of representing machine learning models, where nodes represent operations or computations, and edges represent the tensors flowing between them. This format is easily adaptable to various frameworks which also represent models as graphs.\n\n- **Tools and Ecosystem**: There is a rich ecosystem of tools around ONNX that assist in model conversion, visualization, and optimization. These tools make it easier for developers to work with ONNX models and to convert models between different frameworks seamlessly.\n\n## Common Usage of ONNX\n\nBefore we jump into how to export YOLO26 models to the ONNX format, let's take a look at where ONNX models are usually used.\n\n### CPU Deployment\n\nONNX models are often deployed on CPUs due to their compatibility with ONNX Runtime. This runtime is optimized for CPU execution. It significantly improves inference speed and makes real-time CPU deployments feasible.\n\n### Supported Deployment Options\n\nWhile ONNX models are commonly used on CPUs, they can also be deployed on the following platforms:\n\n- **GPU Acceleration**: ONNX fully supports GPU acceleration, particularly NVIDIA CUDA. For native training and inference on AMD GPUs, use [PyTorch ROCm](amd.md); an ONNX file still requires an AMD-compatible runtime.\n\n- **Edge and Mobile Devices**: ONNX extends to edge and mobile devices, perfect for on-device and real-time inference scenarios. It's lightweight and compatible with edge hardware, and serves as the basis for vendor NPU formats such as [Huawei Ascend](ascend.md), [Qualcomm QNN](qnn.md) for Snapdragon devices, and [RKNN](rockchip-rknn.md) for Rockchip NPUs.\n\n- **Web Browsers**: ONNX can run directly in web browsers, powering interactive and dynamic web-based AI applications.\n\n## Supported Tasks\n\nONNX export supports all seven Ultralytics tasks. Semantic segmentation and depth estimation are available only with YOLO26, the only family that ships those heads.\n\n| Task                             | [YOLOv8](../models/yolov8.md)                                                                                    | [YOLO11](../models/yolo11.md)                                                                                    | [YOLO26](../models/yolo26.md)                                                                                    |\n| :------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| [Detect](../tasks/detect.md)     | ✅     | ✅     | ✅     |\n| [Segment](../tasks/segment.md)   | ✅   | ✅   | ✅   |\n| [Semantic](../tasks/semantic.md) | ❌                                                                                                               | ❌                                                                                                               | ✅ |\n| [Depth](../tasks/depth.md)       | ❌                                                                                                               | ❌                                                                                                               | ✅       |\n| [Classify](../tasks/classify.md) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |\n| [Pose](../tasks/pose.md)         | ✅         | ✅         | ✅         |\n| [OBB](../tasks/obb.md)           | ✅           | ✅           | ✅           |\n\n\n## Exporting YOLO26 Models to ONNX\n\nYou can expand model compatibility and deployment flexibility by converting YOLO26 models to ONNX format. [Ultralytics YOLO26](../models/yolo26.md) provides a straightforward export process that can significantly enhance your model's performance across different platforms.\n\n### Installation\n\nTo install the required package, run:\n\n!!! tip \"Installation\"\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Install the required package for YOLO26\n        pip install ultralytics\n        ```\n\nFor detailed instructions and best practices related to the installation process, check our [YOLO26 Installation guide](../quickstart.md). While installing the required packages for YOLO26, if you encounter any difficulties, consult our [Common Issues guide](../guides/yolo-common-issues.md) for solutions and tips.\n\n### Usage\n\nBefore diving into the usage instructions, be sure to check out the range of [YOLO26 models offered by Ultralytics](../models/index.md). This will help you choose the most appropriate model for your project requirements.\n\nThe ONNX format supports the [Export](../modes/export.md), [Predict](../modes/predict.md), and [Validate](../modes/val.md) modes. Export your model, then load the exported model to run inference or validate its accuracy.\n\n!!! example \"Export\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load a YOLO26 model\n        model = YOLO(\"yolo26n.pt\")\n\n        # Export the model to ONNX format\n        model.export(format=\"onnx\")  # creates 'yolo26n.onnx'\n\n        # Export an INT8-quantized ONNX model with calibration data\n        model.export(format=\"onnx\", quantize=8, data=\"coco8.yaml\")  # creates 'yolo26n_int8.onnx'\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Export a YOLO26n PyTorch model to ONNX format\n        yolo export model=yolo26n.pt format=onnx # creates 'yolo26n.onnx'\n\n        # Export an INT8-quantized ONNX model with calibration data\n        yolo export model=yolo26n.pt format=onnx quantize=8 data=coco8.yaml # creates 'yolo26n_int8.onnx'\n        ```\n\n!!! example \"Predict\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load the exported ONNX model\n        model = YOLO(\"yolo26n.onnx\")\n\n        # Run inference\n        results = model(\"https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg\")\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Run inference with the exported ONNX model\n        yolo predict model=yolo26n.onnx source='https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg'\n        ```\n\n!!! example \"Validate\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load the exported ONNX model\n        model = YOLO(\"yolo26n.onnx\")\n\n        # Validate accuracy on the COCO8 dataset\n        metrics = model.val(data=\"coco8.yaml\")\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Validate the exported ONNX model\n        yolo val model=yolo26n.onnx data=coco8.yaml\n        ```\n\n### Export Arguments\n\nWhen exporting your YOLO26 model to ONNX format, you can customize the process using various arguments to optimize for your specific deployment needs:\n\n| Argument   | Type             | Default  | Description                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |\n| ---------- | ---------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `format`   | `str`            | `'onnx'` | Target format for the exported model, defining compatibility with various deployment environments.                                                                                                                              |\n| `imgsz`    | `int` or `tuple` | `640`    | Desired image size for the model input. Can be an integer for square images or a tuple `(height, width)` for specific dimensions.                                                                                               |\n| `quantize` | `int` or `str`   | `None`   | Quantization precision: `16` (FP16) or `8` (INT8 static quantization with ONNX Runtime using calibration images from `data`, producing an `_int8.onnx` model); `32`/unset is FP32. Replaces the deprecated `half`/`int8` flags. |\n| `data`     | `str`            | `None`   | Dataset YAML used for INT8 calibration; classification instead takes a dataset directory or a built-in dataset name. If omitted with `quantize=8`, Ultralytics selects the default calibration dataset for the model task.      |\n| `fraction` | `float`          | `1.0`    | Fraction of calibration images to use for INT8 quantization.                                                                                                                                                                    |\n| `dynamic`  | `bool`           | `False`  | Allows dynamic input sizes, enhancing flexibility in handling varying image dimensions.                                                                                                                                         |\n| `simplify` | `bool`           | `True`   | Simplifies the model graph with `onnxslim`, potentially improving performance and compatibility.                                                                                                                                |\n| `opset`    | `int`            | `None`   | Specifies the ONNX opset version for compatibility with different ONNX parsers and runtimes. If not set, uses the latest supported version.                                                                                     |\n| `nms`      | `bool`           | `False`  | Adds Non-Maximum Suppression (NMS), essential for accurate and efficient detection post-processing.                                                                                                                             |\n| `batch`    | `int`            | `1`      | Specifies export model batch inference size or the max number of images the exported model will process concurrently in `predict` mode.                                                                                         |\n| `device`   | `str`            | `None`   | Specifies the device for exporting: GPU (`device=0`), CPU (`device=cpu`), MPS for Apple silicon (`device=mps`).                                                                                                                 |\n\nFor more details about the export process, visit the [Ultralytics documentation page on exporting](../modes/export.md).\n\n## Deploying Exported YOLO26 ONNX Models\n\nOnce you've successfully exported your Ultralytics YOLO26 models to ONNX format, the next step is deploying these models in various environments. For detailed instructions on deploying your ONNX models, take a look at the following resources:\n\n- **[ONNX Runtime Python API Documentation](https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/api/python/api_summary.html)**: This guide provides essential information for loading and running ONNX models using ONNX Runtime.\n\n- **[Deploying on Edge Devices](https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/tutorials/iot-edge/)**: Check out this docs page for different examples of deploying ONNX models on edge.\n\n- **[ONNX Tutorials on GitHub](https://github.com/onnx/tutorials)**: A collection of comprehensive tutorials that cover various aspects of using and implementing ONNX models in different scenarios.\n\n- **[Triton Inference Server](../guides/triton-inference-server.md)**: Learn how to deploy your ONNX models with NVIDIA's Triton Inference Server for high-performance, scalable deployments.\n\n## Summary\n\nIn this guide, you've learned how to export Ultralytics YOLO26 models to ONNX format to increase their interoperability and performance across various platforms. You were also introduced to the ONNX Runtime and ONNX deployment options.\n\nONNX export is just one of many [export formats](../modes/export.md) supported by Ultralytics YOLO26, allowing you to deploy your models in virtually any environment. Depending on your specific needs, you might also want to explore other export options like [TensorRT](../integrations/tensorrt.md) for maximum GPU performance or [CoreML](../integrations/coreml.md) for Apple devices.\n\nFor further details on usage, visit the [ONNX official documentation](https://onnx.ai/onnx/intro/).\n\nAlso, if you'd like to know more about other Ultralytics YOLO26 integrations, visit our [integration guide page](../integrations/index.md). You'll find plenty of useful resources and insights there.\n\n## do I export YOLO26 models to ONNX format using Ultralytics?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"To export your YOLO26 models to ONNX format using Ultralytics, follow these steps: For more details, visit the export documentation.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"What are the advantages of using ONNX Runtime for deploying YOLO26 models?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"Using ONNX Runtime for deploying YOLO26 models offers several advantages: Cross-platform compatibility: ONNX Runtime supports various platforms, such as Windows, macOS, and Linux, ensuring your models run smoothly across different environments. Hardware acceleration: ONNX Runtime can leverage hardware-specific optimizations for CPUs, GPUs, and dedicated accelerators, providing high-performance inference. Framework interoperability: Models trained in popular frameworks like PyTorch or TensorFlow can be easily converted to ONNX format and run using ONNX Runtime. Performance optimization: ONNX Runtime can provide up to 3x CPU speedup compared to native PyTorch models, making it ideal for deployment scenarios where GPU resources are limited. Learn more by checking the ONNX Runtime documentation.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"What deployment options are available for YOLO26 models exported to ONNX?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"YOLO26 models exported to ONNX can be deployed on various platforms including: CPUs: Utilizing ONNX Runtime for optimized CPU inference. GPUs: Leveraging NVIDIA CUDA for high-performance GPU acceleration. Edge devices: Running lightweight models on edge and mobile devices for real-time, on-device inference. Web browsers: Executing models directly within web browsers for interactive web-based applications. Cloud services: Deploying on cloud platforms that support ONNX format for scalable inference. For more information, explore our guide on model deployment options.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"Why should I use ONNX format for Ultralytics YOLO26 models?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"Using ONNX format for Ultralytics YOLO26 models provides numerous benefits: Interoperability: ONNX allows models to be transferred between different machine learning frameworks seamlessly. Performance Optimization: ONNX Runtime can enhance model performance by utilizing hardware-specific optimizations. Flexibility: ONNX supports various deployment environments, enabling you to use the same model on different platforms without modification. Standardization: ONNX provides a standardized format that is widely supported across the industry, ensuring long-term compatibility. Refer to the comprehensive guide on exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"How can I troubleshoot issues when exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"When exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX, you might encounter common issues such as mismatched dependencies or unsupported operations. To troubleshoot these problems: Verify that you have the correct version of required dependencies installed. Check the official ONNX documentation for supported operators and features. Review the error messages for clues and consult the Ultralytics Common Issues guide. Try using different export arguments like simplify=True or adjusting the opset version. For dynamic input size issues, set dynamic=True during export. If issues persist, contact Ultralytics support for further assistance.\"}}]}37:[[\"$\",\"script\",null,{\"type\":\"application/ld+json\",\"children\":\"$3c\"}],\"$L3d\"]\n38:[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"mt-16 space-y-3 text-right text-sm gap-3\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L54\",\"how-do-i-export-yolo26-models-to-onnx-format-using-ultralytics\",{\"answerId\":\"how-do-i-export-yolo26-models-to-onnx-format-using-ultralytics-answer\",\"id\":\"how-do-i-export-yolo26-models-to-onnx-format-using-ultralytics\",\"question\":\"How do I export YOLO26 models to ONNX format using Ultralytics?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/onnx.md\",\"children\":\"To export your YOLO26 models to ONNX format using Ultralytics, follow these steps:\\n\\n!!! example \\\"Usage\\\"\\n\\n    === \\\"Python\\\"\\n\\n        ```python\\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\\n\\n        # Load a YOLO26 model\\n        model = YOLO(\\\"yolo26n.pt\\\")\\n\\n        # Export the model to ONNX format\\n        model.export(format=\\\"onnx\\\")  # creates 'yolo26n.onnx'\\n\\n        # Load the exported ONNX model\\n        onnx_model = YOLO(\\\"yolo26n.onnx\\\")\\n\\n        # Run inference\\n        results = onnx_model(\\\"https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg\\\")\\n        ```\\n\\n    === \\\"CLI\\\"\\n\\n        ```bash\\n        # Export a YOLO26n PyTorch model to ONNX format\\n        yolo export model=yolo26n.pt format=onnx # creates 'yolo26n.onnx'\\n\\n        # Run inference with the exported model\\n        yolo predict model=yolo26n.onnx source='https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg'\\n        ```\\n\\nFor more details, visit the [export documentation](../modes/export.md).\"}]}],[\"$\",\"$L54\",\"what-are-the-advantages-of-using-onnx-runtime-for-deploying-yolo26-models\",{\"answerId\":\"what-are-the-advantages-of-using-onnx-runtime-for-deploying-yolo26-models-answer\",\"id\":\"what-are-the-advantages-of-using-onnx-runtime-for-deploying-yolo26-models\",\"question\":\"What are the advantages of using ONNX Runtime for deploying YOLO26 models?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/onnx.md\",\"children\":\"Using ONNX Runtime for deploying YOLO26 models offers several advantages:\\n\\n- **Cross-platform compatibility**: ONNX Runtime supports various platforms, such as Windows, macOS, and Linux, ensuring your models run smoothly across different environments.\\n- **Hardware acceleration**: ONNX Runtime can leverage hardware-specific optimizations for CPUs, GPUs, and dedicated accelerators, providing high-performance inference.\\n- **Framework interoperability**: Models trained in popular frameworks like [PyTorch](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/pytorch) or TensorFlow can be easily converted to ONNX format and run using ONNX Runtime.\\n- **Performance optimization**: ONNX Runtime can provide up to 3x CPU speedup compared to native PyTorch models, making it ideal for deployment scenarios where GPU resources are limited.\\n\\nLearn more by checking the [ONNX Runtime documentation](https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/api/python/api_summary.html).\"}]}],[\"$\",\"$L54\",\"what-deployment-options-are-available-for-yolo26-models-exported-to-onnx\",{\"answerId\":\"what-deployment-options-are-available-for-yolo26-models-exported-to-onnx-answer\",\"id\":\"what-deployment-options-are-available-for-yolo26-models-exported-to-onnx\",\"question\":\"What models exported to ONNX can be deployed on various platforms including:\\n\\n- **CPUs**: Utilizing ONNX Runtime for optimized CPU inference.\\n- **GPUs**: Leveraging NVIDIA CUDA for high-performance GPU acceleration.\\n- **Edge devices**: Running lightweight models on edge and mobile devices for real-time, on-device inference.\\n- **Web browsers**: Executing models directly within web browsers for interactive web-based applications.\\n- **Cloud services**: Deploying on cloud platforms that support ONNX format for scalable inference.\\n\\nFor more information, explore our guide on [model deployment options](../guides/model-deployment-options.md).\"}]\n56:[\"$\",\"$L54\",\"why-should-i-use-onnx-format-for-ultralytics-yolo26-models\",{\"answerId\":\"why-should-i-use-onnx-format-for-ultralytics-yolo26-models-answer\",\"id\":\"why-should-i-use-onnx-format-for-ultralytics-yolo26-models\",\"question\":\"Why should I use ONNX format for Ultralytics YOLO26 models?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/onnx.md\",\"children\":\"Using ONNX format for Ultralytics YOLO26 models provides numerous benefits:\\n\\n- **Interoperability**: ONNX allows models to be transferred between different machine learning frameworks seamlessly.\\n- **Performance Optimization**: ONNX Runtime can enhance model performance by utilizing hardware-specific optimizations.\\n- **Flexibility**: ONNX supports various deployment environments, enabling you to use the same model on different platforms without modification.\\n- **Standardization**: ONNX provides a standardized format that is widely supported across the industry, ensuring long-term compatibility.\\n\\nRefer to the comprehensive guide on [exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX](../integrations/onnx.md).\"}]}]\n57:[\"$\",\"$L54\",\"how-can-i-troubleshoot-issues-when-exporting-yolo26-models-to-onnx\",{\"answerId\":\"how-can-i-troubleshoot-issues-when-exporting-yolo26-models-to-onnx-answer\",\"id\":\"how-can-i-troubleshoot-issues-when-exporting-yolo26-models-to-onnx\",\"question\":\"How can I troubleshoot issues when exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/onnx.md\",\"children\":\"When exporting YOLO26 models to ONNX, you might encounter common issues such as mismatched dependencies or unsupported operations. To troubleshoot these problems:\\n\\n1. Verify that you have the correct version of required dependencies installed.\\n2. Check the official [ONNX documentation](https://onnx.ai/onnx/intro/) for supported operators and features.\\n3. Review the error messages for clues and consult the [Ultralytics Common Issues guide](../guides/yolo-common-issues.md).\\n4. Try using different export arguments like `simplify=True` or adjusting the `opset` version.\\n5. For dynamic input size issues, set `dynamic=True` during export.\\n\\nIf issues persist, contact Ultralytics support for further assistance.\"}]}]\ne:[[\"$\",\"meta\",\"0\",{\"charSet\":\"utf-8\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"1\",{\"name\":\"viewport\",\"content\":\"width=device-width,
